45:["$","p",null,{"children":["The container needs a host kernel of 5.19 or newer, because the server uses ",["$","code",null,{"children":"io_uring"}],"; see ","$L8e"," if it fails to start. The capabilities, seccomp setting, and memlock limit form a permissive development setup. Production deployments can use narrower syscall permissions and a finite memory budget; see ","$L8f"," for the details. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"IGGY_TCP_ADDRESS"}]," is needed because the server binds to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"127.0.0.1"}]," inside the container by default, which a published port cannot reach. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"IGGY_NODE_ADVERTISED_ADDRESS"}]," is needed because that wildcard leaves the server with no address to give clients, and it refuses to start rather than publish one nobody can dial. Here the port is published to the host, so ",["$","code",null,{"children":"localhost"}]," is that address. Setting the root credentials explicitly means the username and password used later in this guide will work."]}]

48:["$","p",null,{"children":["A bare ",["$","code",null,{"children":"iggy-server"}]," boot does ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"not"}]," create the root user with the well-known ",["$","code",null,{"children":"iggy"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"iggy"}]," pair. It generates a random password and prints it ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"exactly once"}]," to the logs. The ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--with-default-root-credentials"}]," flag switches to the development credentials (username: ",["$","code",null,{"children":"iggy"}],", password: ",["$","code",null,{"children":"iggy"}],"), and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"--fresh"}]," wipes the data directory first, so the root user is created anew with them. You can also set ",["$","code",null,{"children":"IGGY_ROOT_USERNAME"}]," and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"IGGY_ROOT_PASSWORD"}]," yourself. The environment ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"takes precedence"}]," over the flag. The credentials initialize the stored root account ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"only on first creation"}],"; changing them on a later boot does not reset its password. 80:["$","p",null,{"children":["At the first glance, it might look a bit more complicated than ",["$","code",null,{"children":"send_messages"}]," function, so let's discuss the fields one by one:"]}]
81:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"stream_id"}]," - the ID of the stream (numeric or string) from which we want to poll the messages."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"topic_id"}]," - the ID of the topic (numeric or string) from which we want to poll the messages."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"partition_id"}]," - the ID of the partition from which we want to poll the messages. Specify the partition for a regular ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Consumer"}],". For a joined ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ConsumerGroup"}],", pass ",["$","code",null,{"children":"None"}]," to let the SDK select a partition from the assignments it synchronizes with the server. An explicit partition is also accepted, but the server checks that the member owns it."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"consumer"}]," - the type of the consumer (kind + ID), either the default ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Consumer"}]," means the standalone client which does the message polling on its own, independently of the other consumers (unless they would use the same ID), or the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ConsumerGroup"}]," which might be used to create the group of consumers sharing the common identifier - this is especially useful in the case of the horizontal scaling, where we want to ensure, that the same (and only one) consumer, will poll the messages from the specific partition, and there will be no overlap with the other consumers from the same group. For example, when scaling out (by adding more instances) the group of payment processing microservices, we probably don't want the multiple instances to process the same payment. Group assignment coordinates polling, but application side effects still need idempotency across retries and rebalances."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"strategy"}]," - the way in which we want to poll the messages. The default one being ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Offset"}]," (underlying ",["$","code",null,{"children":"PollingKind"}]," enum) means that we will start polling the messages from the particular offset provided in the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"value"}]," field - it's on the client, to keep track of the most recent offset. On the other hand, we could also use the different kind, for example ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Next"}],", which means, that the next messages will be returned to the client, depending on the so-called ",["$","code",null,{"children":"consumer offset"}]," value stored on the server side. The client may not need to track the offset on its own anymore, but instead, call ",["$","code",null,{"children":"store_consumer_offset()"}]," to save it on the server (e.g. after each processed message or the whole batch), or use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"auto_commit: true"}]," for server-managed offset advancement. Auto-commit is best-effort: a poll does not wait for the offset to commit, and a follower-served poll does not replicate it. Messages can be replayed after failure. The offset can also advance before the application processes the response, so use an explicit offset store after processing when that distinction matters."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"count"}]," - amount of the messages that the consumer would like to receive in the single response from the server."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"auto_commit"}]," - whether to request server-managed offset advancement when messages are fetched, with the best-effort behavior described above."]}],"\n"]}],"\n"]}]
